//! §5.6 borrowed systems: normative constant/causal-adjoint entry analysis,
//! followed by an exact symbolic proof on D_F (0.9 pass V; 0.8 erratum).
//!
//! EntryAnalysis retains only constants and exact all-operand adjoint pops;
//! it never imports a polynomial identity from a frame or region prefix.
//! At each frame entry, interface and borrowed bits are independent variables,
//! allocated local constants survive, and other local bits become independent
//! variables. The exact evaluator then uses canonical Boolean algebraic normal
//! forms (ANFs) to decide restoration and independence over this entire D_F.
//! ANF simplification inside a frame cannot strengthen a later entry domain.
//! Loops compose exact frame maps by squaring; the entry analysis traverses
//! visits with complete-state cycle skipping. Quantum/opaque inverse history
//! retains every operand, so an unmatched reader blocks restoration. Opaque
//! applications include their complete inputs; unresolved function-specific
//! identities require a checked §12 borrow_frames claim on the same D_F.
//! Every completed lifetime is checked before later borrowing can cancel it.
//! SPEC-QUESTIONS 20 records the binding interpretation.
use crate::{
    err,
    object::list,
    validation::{gate, gate_operands, normalize_call},
    Error, Function, Instruction, Object, Result, Type,
};
use std::{
    cell::RefCell,
    collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet},
    rc::Rc,
};

pub fn validate_translation_lemma(
    v: &[Instruction],
    w: &[Instruction],
    x: &BTreeSet<String>,
    borrowed: &BTreeSet<String>,
) -> Result<()> {
    if !x.is_disjoint(borrowed) {
        return err("§5.6", "translation lemma requires X disjoint from B");
    }
    let flips = |sequence: &[Instruction]| -> Result<Vec<(Vec<String>, String)>> {
        sequence
            .iter()
            .map(|i| {
                if !["x", "cx", "ccx", "mcx"].contains(&i.op.as_str()) {
                    return err("§5.6", "translation lemma contains a non-X-family gate");
                }
                let (c, t, _, _) = gate_operands(i)?;
                Ok((c, t[0].clone()))
            })
            .collect()
    };
    let v = flips(v)?;
    let w = flips(w)?;
    let controls: BTreeSet<_> = w.iter().flat_map(|(c, _)| c.iter().cloned()).collect();
    for (c, t) in v {
        if !borrowed.contains(&t) || c.iter().any(|c| !x.contains(c)) {
            return err("§5.6", "V must target only B with controls only in X");
        }
    }
    for (c, t) in w {
        if x.contains(&t) || borrowed.contains(&t) || controls.contains(&t) {
            return err("§5.6", "W target overlaps X, B, or W's controls");
        }
        if c.iter().filter(|c| borrowed.contains(*c)).count() > 1 {
            return err("§5.6", "W gate has more than one borrowed control");
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// An ANF monomial: sorted, distinct variable ids (the empty monomial is 1).
type Mono = Vec<u32>;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Default)]
struct Poly(BTreeSet<Mono>);
impl Poly {
    fn zero() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }
    fn one() -> Self {
        Self(BTreeSet::from([Vec::new()]))
    }
    fn var(v: u32) -> Self {
        Self(BTreeSet::from([vec![v]]))
    }
    fn xor(&self, o: &Self) -> Self {
        Self(self.0.symmetric_difference(&o.0).cloned().collect())
    }
    fn and(&self, o: &Self) -> Self {
        let mut out = BTreeSet::new();
        for a in &self.0 {
            for b in &o.0 {
                let mut m: Mono = a.iter().chain(b).copied().collect();
                m.sort_unstable();
                m.dedup();
                if !out.insert(m.clone()) {
                    out.remove(&m);
                }
            }
        }
        Self(out)
    }
    fn mentions(&self, vars: &BTreeSet<u32>) -> bool {
        self.0.iter().flatten().any(|v| vars.contains(v))
    }
}

/// A compiled operation on named wires.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Node {
    /// Target ^= AND of controls (with polarity).
    Flip(Vec<(String, bool)>, String),
    Invalid(Error),
    NeedsClaim(Error),
}
impl From<Error> for FrameFailure {
    fn from(e: Error) -> Self {
        Self::Invalid(e)
    }
}
/// One causal operation, present on every operand stack. It cancels only when
/// it is on top of ALL its operand stacks, preserving outstanding readers.
/// Saved polynomials preserve correlations; unknown never invents zero.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct InverseFact {
    gate: Node,
    before: BTreeMap<String, Poly>,
}
/// Normative §5.6 entry analysis, independent of the exact frame evaluator.
/// None denotes both basis values; no Boolean expression is retained here.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct EntryAnalysis {
    values: BTreeMap<String, Option<bool>>,
    stacks: BTreeMap<String, Vec<EntryInverse>>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
struct EntryInverse {
    node: Node,
    before: BTreeMap<String, Option<bool>>,
}
impl EntryAnalysis {
    fn value(&self, w: &str) -> Option<bool> {
        self.values.get(w).copied().flatten()
    }
    fn condition(&self, controls: &[(String, bool)]) -> Option<bool> {
        if controls.iter().any(|(w, p)| self.value(w) == Some(!p)) {
            Some(false)
        } else if controls.iter().all(|(w, p)| self.value(w) == Some(*p)) {
            Some(true)
        } else {
            None
        }
    }
    fn barrier(&mut self, touched: &BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut affected = touched.clone();
        loop {
            let old = affected.len();
            for w in affected.clone() {
                if let Some(stack) = self.stacks.get(&w) {
                    for event in stack {
                        event.node.wires(&mut affected);
                    }
                }
            }
            if affected.len() == old {
                break;
            }
        }
        for w in affected {
            self.stacks.remove(&w);
        }
    }
    fn apply(&mut self, node: &Node) {
        if let Node::Repeat(n, body) = node {
            // Exact finite traversal; identical complete abstract states permit
            // cycle skipping. This has no widening or polynomial shortcut.
            let mut seen = Vec::new();
            let mut i = 0;
            while i < *n {
                if let Some((previous, _)) = seen.iter().find(|(_, state)| state == self) {
                    let period = i - previous;
                    let skip = (*n - i) / period * period;
                    if skip != 0 {
                        i += skip;
                        continue;
                    }
                }
                seen.push((i, self.clone()));
                for n in body {
                    self.apply(n);
                }
                i += 1;
            }
            return;
        }
        if let Node::Call {
            controls,
            body: Some(body),
            ..
        } = node
        {
            if self.condition(controls) != Some(false) {
                for n in body {
                    self.apply(n);
                }
            }
            return;
        }
        let mut touched = BTreeSet::new();
        node.wires(&mut touched);
        let controls = match node {
            Node::Flip(c, _) | Node::Swap(c, _, _) => Some(c),
            Node::Opaque { controls, .. }
            | Node::Quantum { controls, .. }
            | Node::Call { controls, .. } => Some(controls),
            _ => None,
        };
        if controls.is_some_and(|c| self.condition(c) == Some(false)) {
            return;
        }
        let reversible = matches!(
            node,
            Node::Flip(..) | Node::Swap(..) | Node::Opaque { .. } | Node::Quantum { .. }
        );
        if reversible {
            let saved = touched
                .iter()
                .next()
                .and_then(|w| self.stacks.get(w))
                .and_then(|s| s.last())
                .cloned();
            if let Some(saved) = saved {
                if saved.node.adjoint() == *node
//! Council round 1: codex B14, muse B6 (§5.6 has no 16-wire limit).
mod support;
use bennett_isa_ref::{Object, QuantumBindings, QuantumState};

fn perm_action(width: u64, body: &str, bounds: &[u64]) -> String {
    let values: Vec<String> = bounds.iter().map(u64::to_string).collect();
    let signature = format!(
        r#"{{"abi":"0.8","args":[],"bounds":[{}],"in":[1],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[{width}],"tier":"q"}}"#,
        values.join(",")
    );
    let regs = if bounds.is_empty() {
        String::new()
    } else {
        format!("regs {{  }} bounds {{{}}}\n", values.join(", "))
    };
    support::object(
        &[(
            "a",
            &signature,
            &format!("perm_action @a (t:wire[{width}] mut) (x:wire in) () () ()\n{regs}^entry():\nb = qborrow any 1\n{body}\nqreturn b"),
        )],
        "{}",
        "",
    )
}
/// Runs @a with t = `t`, x = `x`, borrowed b = `b`; returns (t, b).
fn run(o: &Object, width: usize, t: u128, x: u128, b: u128) -> (u128, u128) {
    let mut q = QuantumState::new();
    let tw = q.allocate(width).unwrap();
    let xw = q.allocate(1).unwrap();
    let bw = q.allocate(1).unwrap();
    for (wires, value) in [(&tw, t), (&xw, x), (&bw, b)] {
        for (i, w) in wires.iter().enumerate() {
            if value >> i & 1 == 1 {
                q.gate("x", &[*w], None, &[]).unwrap();
            }
        }
    }
    let mut bindings = QuantumBindings::default();
    bindings.bundles.insert("t".into(), tw.clone());
    bindings.bundles.insert("x".into(), xw.clone());
    bindings.borrowed.push(bw.clone());
    o.execute_action("a", &mut q, &bindings, false, &[])
        .unwrap();
    (q.read_basis(&tw).unwrap(), q.read_basis(&bw).unwrap())
}
fn swaps17() -> String {
    let forward: Vec<String> = (0..16).map(|i| format!("swap b t[{i}]")).collect();
    let back: Vec<String> = (0..16).rev().map(|i| format!("swap b t[{i}]")).collect();
    [forward, back].concat().join("\n")
}

#[test]
fn codex_b14_seventeen_wire_swap_identity_frame() {
    let o = Object::parse(&perm_action(16, &swaps17(), &[])).unwrap();
    // Sample the 2^17 domain; the ignored sweep below enumerates all of it.
    for k in 0..512u128 {
        let (t, b) = ((k * 2654435761) & 0xffff, k & 1);
        assert_eq!(run(&o, 16, t, 0, b), (t, b));
    }
}
#[test]
#[ignore = "exhaustive 2^17 execution of the B14 frame; run with --ignored"]
fn codex_b14_seventeen_wire_swap_identity_frame_exhaustive() {
    let o = Object::parse(&perm_action(16, &swaps17(), &[])).unwrap();
    for t in 0..1u128 << 16 {
        for b in 0..2 {
            assert_eq!(run(&o, 16, t, 0, b), (t, b));
        }
    }
}
#[test]
fn muse_b6_eighteen_wire_paired_cx_frame_and_its_dependent_variant() {
    let pairs = |skip_first: bool| {
        (0..17)
            .flat_map(|i| {
                let one = format!("cx b t[{i}]");
                if i == 0 && skip_first {
                    vec![one]
                } else {
                    vec![one.clone(), one]
                }
            })
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            .join("\n")
    };
    let o = Object::parse(&perm_action(17, &pairs(false), &[])).unwrap();
    for k in 0..256u128 {
        let (t, b) = ((k * 40503) & 0x1ffff, k >> 7 & 1);
        assert_eq!(run(&o, 17, t, 1, b), (t, b));
    }
    // cx b t[0] once: t[0] ^= b depends on the borrowed entry value.
    let e = Object::parse(&perm_action(17, &pairs(true), &[]))
        .unwrap_err()
        .to_string();
    assert!(e.contains("§5.6"), "{e}");
}
#[test]
fn borrow_frames_inside_bounded_loops_are_composed_not_unrolled() {
    // (cx b t[0])^(2^40) = I; the former verifier refused loops above 10^6.
    let o = Object::parse(&perm_action(
        1,
        "qloop 1099511627776 {\ncx b t\n}",
        &[1099511627776],
    ))
    .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(run(&o, 1, 1, 0, 1), (1, 1));
    let e = Object::parse(&perm_action(1, "qloop 3 {\ncx b t\n}", &[3]))
        .unwrap_err()
        .to_string();
    assert!(e.contains("§5.6"), "{e}");
    // The order-3 linear map (b,t) -> (t, b xor t), cubed, restores both wires.
    Object::parse(&perm_action(1, "qloop 3 {\ncx b t\ncx t b\n}", &[3])).unwrap();
    Object::parse(&perm_action(1, "qloop 2 {\ncx b t\ncx t b\n}", &[2])).unwrap_err();
}

#[test]
fn r2_opaque_borrow_calls_bind_classical_args_and_tokens_through_inlining() {
    use bennett_isa_ref::{Argument, Budget};
    let os = r#"{"abi":"0.8","args":["i1"],"fault":4,"in":[],"kind":"oracle","mem":[],"tgt":1,"tier":"q"}"#;
    let ps = r#"{"abi":"0.8","args":["token","i1","i1"],"bounds":[],"in":[],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[4],"tier":"q"}"#;
    let cs = r#"{"abi":"0.8","args":["token","i1"],"bounds":[],"in":[],"kind":"perm_action","mem":[],"mut":[1,4],"tier":"q"}"#;
    let oracle = "oracle @o () (t:wire) (f:wire[4]) () (n:i1)\n^entry():\nlift.retval n";
    let child = "perm_action @child (d:wire mut, f:wire[4] mut) () () (token:token, value:i1) ()\n^entry():\noracle_call @o token () d f () (value)";
    for inline in [false, true] {
        let call = |v| {
            if inline {
                format!("action_call @child (d, f) () () (tk, {v})")
            } else {
                format!("oracle_call @o tk () d f () ({v})")
            }
        };
        let build = |second| {
            let body = format!("perm_action @a (f:wire[4] mut) () () (tk:token, x:i1, y:i1) ()\n^entry():\nd = qborrow any 1\n{}\n{}\nqreturn d", call("x"), call(second));
            support::object(
                &[("o", os, oracle), ("child", cs, child), ("a", ps, &body)],
                "{}",
                "",
            )
        };
        let e = Object::parse(&build("y")).expect_err("x and y are not the same classical binding");
        assert!(e.to_string().contains("§5.6"), "{e}");
        let o = Object::parse(&build("x")).unwrap();
        // d_out=d_in XOR x XOR x=d_in; fault XOR 0 twice, for every f,d.
        for x in 0..2 {
            for f in 0..16 {
                for d in 0..2 {

def tensor_ok(entries, operation, b_index):
    seen = {}
    for entry in entries:
        output, phase = operation(entry)
        if output[b_index] "'!= entry[b_index] or phase != 1:
            return False
        other_in = entry[:b_index] + entry[b_index+1:]
        other_out = output[:b_index] + output[b_index+1:]
        if other_in in seen and seen[other_in] != other_out:
            return False
        seen[other_in] = other_out
    return True

# v2v — frame descriptors for every realised invocation context

Council proposal for `b-isa-spec-v2v` (P1).

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §12.1 “Invocation contexts” includes every reached invocation,
including defaults, with resolved arguments, budget and choices. §12.3
stores one region record per closure hash and requires one B5 frame.
§9.4 requires each slot's concrete target class and substitutes parameters
and budget into width constants before forming c-only expressions.
The same callee can receive A in one call and B in another; no single
descriptor can express both. A `(variant,controls)` key alone also fails
when those values agree. This is a C4/C5 contract defect.

North Star §§4.2/4.4/5.1 requires freely nested regions and independently
checkable schedules/certificates. Refusing repeated mixed-class calls
would narrow this machine. [4ta](b-isa-spec-4ta.md) describes a different
case: a single slot fragmented within one context. Both repairs compose.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Use the source from `../bennett-sched/tests/pipeline.rs`, test
`context_varying_frame_classes_are_explicitly_deferred_instead_of_misreported`:
root `pair(t,u)` calls the same one-bit `flip` on t and then u; flip is X.
Place t in A and u in B, capacity 1 each. Each call is forward with zero
inherited controls and the same closure hash. Callee frame one has
`mut:0,class:A,width:1`; callee frame two has B instead.

For all four root basis inputs, the result is `(t XOR 1,u XOR 1)`.
At unit duration/rate the root time is 2 and each root interface bit is
live for both slots: occupancy[A]=occupancy[B]=2, peaks 1 each.
The per-invocation callee views have duration 1 and just their respective
class. Neither copying A into both records nor taking a class wildcard
describes the realised placement. This witness needs no heterogeneous
slot and no nonzero epsilon.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Duplicate region records or rename the callee | Breaks closure-keyed identity or unnecessarily changes the source. |
| Use one wildcard/maximal frame | Cannot verify concrete classes and actual widths. |
| Exact context-indexed frames, uniform shorthand retained | Describes both calls and keeps old uniform certificates. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Replace the §12.3 region `frame (required)` entry by:

```text
frame?: <§9.4 descriptor>,
frame_contexts?: [{context_key:<§12.1 key>, frame:<§9.4 descriptor>}]
```

Append:

```text
Each scheduled region record contains exactly one of frame and
frame_contexts. frame is a descriptor valid for every reached invocation
of that region in the bound root realisation, after instantiating c for
each invocation. It may be used only if all its concrete classes and
resolved non-c-dependent widths describe every invocation truthfully.
frame_contexts contains exactly one record for each reached invocation,
in increasing bytewise order of the JCS encoding of context_key. The
key is exactly §12.1's fully resolved invocation chain; no wildcard,
variant/control-only key, omitted default-covered call or repeated key
is admitted. The root invocation has key []. Resolve its variant,
controls, arguments, budget, placements and expansion choices from the
bound invocation tree, never from record order or a guessed root budget.

Select the context's descriptor before substituting c and check its
slots, classes, widths, allocation identities and lifetimes against
that invocation. Inherited controls remain caller-owned and separate
from callee slots. Distinct contexts have distinct dynamic frame
identities even when their static descriptor id strings agree. Identical
descriptor bytes do not authorize shared physical storage; §9.4's
live-range and disjointness rules remain required. A standalone reference
record uses frame for its explicitly bound invocation or a descriptor
proved uniform over its declared invocation scope.
```

Here `context_key` uses the same spelling as the first-pass resource and
fault proposals. Their arrays and `frame_contexts` must cover the same
reached context set when applicable, rather than enumerate independently.
The originating bead's field spelling `context` is superseded by this
consistent proposed spelling; neither is published yet.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No callee clone, source/interface/closure hash or catalog change is
required. Certificate bytes change. Existing schedules already distinguish
the two source call points; no new call edge is invented. Schedulers,
specialisers and checkers must retain full context keys across linked and
default-covered calls. Estimator frames and resource reports must select
the same context. A uniformly valid c-expression stays usable as `frame`;
the repair must not force needless duplication of uniform certificates.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the pair/flip case; check both class records and all four outputs,
then inverse restoration. Reject two A records, a missing default call,
duplicate/foreign key, both `frame` and `frame_contexts`, neither field,
and a width from another token budget at the same variant/control count.
Add a homogeneous two-call control accepted with old `frame`, and combine
one context with 4ta fragments. Finite output/resource arithmetic is
checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The sibling test was read, not run; its
current `Unsupported` expectation documents the gap, not a completed fix.

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the ISA is

A program model for classical, reversible and coherent computation with
one semantics, executed by the VM (`bennett-vm`) and the circuit
specialiser (`bennett-circuits`), produced by the plugin
(`bennett-plugin`) through the dialect's reference semantics, scheduled
through §11, described for hardware by target descriptions (§8). Nothing
here refers to LLVM.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Executor | VM, specialiser, hardware stacks | §3–§7, §14 exactly, and the checking of inverse-pair claims (§4.5, §12.1; a certificate applies when its `closure_hash` binding equals the region's); suites A–E, H |
| Producer | plugin, library compilers | well-formed objects (§9, §16) and regions (§5, §6); suite F |
| Scheduler | `bennett-sched` and competitors | §11; only schedules valid under §6; suite H |
| Checker | `bennett-conformance` | validates certificates (§12) without trusting producers |
| Target author | vendors | target descriptions (§8); suite E |

### 1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*

Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of
§14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the
memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor,
accessor and destructor is listed). **Minimality claims** (north star
§5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed
in §17.

## 2. Terminology

- **Tier.** CC, R, Q. **Wire**, **bundle** `wire[N]` (little-endian).
- **Location** `reg[N]`: a named mutable cell of an oracle or lproc
  register file. **Pseudo-location**: an oracle interface input or
  classical argument, or an lproc classical parameter (§3.7), readable
  only in its declaring function, never written.
- **Region**: a Q function with a declared interface: **lifted oracle**
  (§5.2) or **quantum action** (§5.3); **permutation action** (§5.3).
- **lproc**: a lifted procedure with its own params and locations; it
  resolves names only to its own params and locations (§3.7).
- **Budget**: classical (B, heap_limit, stack_limit, depth_limit).
- **Frame**: per machine step: `old_pc`, slots (assignment, capture and
  delivery slots, §3.6), `flag`, `succ`.
- **Basis configuration**: classical values on every component of D.
- **Gate classes.** Permutation: `x cx ccx mcx swap`. Diagonal: `s sdg t
  tdg cz rz`. Non-diagonal: `h rx ry`.
- **Verifying executor**: an executor that can decide exactly whether a
  set of wires is clean on every basis configuration (a state-vector or
  branch-enumeration simulator); every other executor (hardware) is
  **non-verifying**. Rules that depend on the difference say which
  applies (§3.3 `qreg.free`, §10, §17 item 14).
- **Effective control context** at a point: the set of wires on the
  active predicate stack plus the inherited control context (the control
  wires of every enclosing `ctl` and predicate stack of every enclosing
  invocation, transitively); duplicates count once. Its size is the
  **effective control count**.

## 3. Machine model

### 3.1 Tiers and the control invariant

```
  CC ──calls──▶ R functions (call/uncall)       CC ──calls──▶ Q regions
  R  ──calls──▶ R functions, Q regions          Q  ──calls──▶ Q regions (§5.5); oracle bodies call lprocs
```

/// xoshiro256** (Blackman and Vigna), the §12.1 sampler.
pub struct Xoshiro256(pub [u64; 4]);
impl Xoshiro256 {
    pub fn next_word(&mut self) -> u64 {
        let s = &mut self.0;
        let result = s[1].wrapping_mul(5).rotate_left(7).wrapping_mul(9);
        let t = s[1] << 17;
        s[2] ^= s[0];
        s[3] ^= s[1];
        s[1] ^= s[2];
        s[0] ^= s[3];
        s[2] ^= t;
        s[3] = s[3].rotate_left(45);
        result
    }
    /// A uniform value of `width` bits (R = 2^width: no redraws, §12.1).
    pub fn bits(&mut self, width: usize) -> Result<BitVector> {
        let lo = BitVector::zero(width)?;
        let hi = ones(width)?;
        self.draw(width, &lo, &hi)
    }
    /// §12.1: m = ceil(ceil(log2 R)/64) words, least significant first,
    /// reduced modulo 2^ceil(log2 R), redrawn while >= R, plus lo.
    fn draw(&mut self, width: usize, lo: &BitVector, hi: &BitVector) -> Result<BitVector> {
        let range = increment(&hi.binary("sub", lo)?.resize(width + 1, false)?)?;
        let bits = {
            let r_minus_1 = range.binary("sub", &BitVector::from_u128(width + 1, 1)?)?;
            (0..=width)
                .rev()
                .find(|i| r_minus_1.bit(*i).unwrap_or(false))
                .map_or(0, |i| i + 1)
        };
        loop {
            let mut v = BitVector::zero(width + 1)?;
            for word in 0..bits.div_ceil(64) {
                let w = self.next_word();
                for b in 0..64 {
                    let i = word * 64 + b;
